Um... I mean, maybe a little bit? I know everyone makes mistakes at work, so I really try not to make a big deal out of it. I don't want to sound like I'm just complaining. But... lately, I just keep replaying these tiny mistakes I make in the lab. 

And, um, instead of just fixing it and moving on like I should, I start telling myself that... that I'm not just bad at my job, but like, I'm actually a bad person? Um... I try to? I really do try to keep up with things, because I don't want to be a downer for anyone. But... I guess I've been a little bad about my hobbies lately. I hope it's okay to admit that.

I've had to ask my coworkers to double-check my reports, or even take over sections that I used to just breeze through on my own. I hate being a bother to them, they're so busy themselves.

I just don't want to drag them down with my mood, you know? I really am trying to push past it, I promise. Does that answer your question okay? I can give you more examples if you need them.

I don't know, it just feels more like another chore on my to-do list instead of something I actually look forward to. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is, I know everyone goes through phases where they don't feel like doing their hobbies.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're just going through the motions of daily life without any real sense of pleasure or enjoyment?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I think that's exactly it. Like I was just saying with the... the 3D printer and everything? It really does feel a bit like I'm just going through the motions. \n\nI mean, I still do the things I'm supposed to do, I don't want you to think I'm just giving up or anything! But... yeah, looking at that same half-finished figure sitting there for months, or forcing myself to do yoga or walk in the woods... it just feels like another task to check off instead of something I actually want to do. I feel awful even admitting that, because I know I have a good life and I should be enjoying things. Is that what you meant by going through the motions? I really hope I'm explaining it okay for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so? I mean, I haven't really been keeping track, to be honest. I don't weigh myself very often, I hope that's alright. \n\nMy clothes still seem to fit about the same, so I think my weight has been pretty steady. I always try to make sure I eat enough and take care of myself, I don't want you to worry about that. Does that answer your question? I can try to pay more attention to it if you need me to.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that happen in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, maybe a little bit? I know everyone makes mistakes at work, so I really try not to make a big deal out of it. I don't want to sound like I'm just complaining. But... lately, I just keep replaying these tiny mistakes I make in the lab. \n\nAnd, um, instead of just fixing it and moving on like I should, I start telling myself that... that I'm not just bad at my job, but like, I'm actually a bad person? Like I deserve whatever bad consequences come from it. I know that probably sounds like a huge overreaction, and I really do try to do my best! I just feel so awful when I mess up, even over the little things. Is it normal to feel that way? I really hope I'm not being too dramatic about it.\n\nDoctor: Has something traumatic or very stressful happened to you recently?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really? I mean, honestly, my life is pretty stable right now. \n\nI feel almost... I don't know, a little embarrassed admitting that? Because I know I don't really have a good reason to be feeling the way I do. Nothing terrible has happened to me at all. Work has just been the usual lab stuff, nothing crazy, and I'm really lucky to have a safe place to live and everything. \n\nI really don't want you to think I'm just complaining when I have it so good. But yeah, no big traumas or anything like that. I hope it's okay that I don't have a better explanation for you? I promise I'm not trying to hide anything, there just really hasn't been any major stress lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things that you used to find pleasurable, like eating your favorite foods or spending time with loved ones?\n\nPatient: Um... I try to? I really do try to keep up with things, because I don't want to be a downer for anyone. But... I guess I've been a little bad about my hobbies lately. I hope it's okay to admit that. \n\nLike, my 3D printer for my miniatures... it's actually been sitting there with the exact same half-finished figure on it for months now. I keep telling myself I'll finish it, I promise I haven't completely given up! But even staying active, like doing yoga or just going for a walk in the woods... it all just feels like another task I have to check off my list, instead of something I actually want to do. \n\nI feel kind of awful about it, honestly, because I know I should be enjoying those things. Should I be forcing myself to do them anyway? I can definitely try harder to get back into my routine if you think that's what I should be doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're worthless or a failure as a person?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. I guess it kind of goes back to what I was saying earlier? I'm sorry to repeat myself, I hope it's okay that I'm bringing it up again. \n\nBut... yeah, when I keep replaying those tiny mistakes from the lab, it really does turn into me telling myself that I'm not just bad at my job, but... but that I'm actually a bad person? Like a complete failure, and that I deserve whatever bad consequences come from it. \n\nI know it's not a very productive way to think, and I promise I really try to push those thoughts away so I can be better! I just... it's hard to shake that feeling sometimes. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it okay.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um... let's see. I guess it's been a while now. I hope it's not too bad that I let it go on for so long before getting help? I just kept thinking I could handle it on my own, and I didn't want to bother anyone.\n\nBut, um, I think it really started when I got moved into the prototype-testing role at work. That was... gosh, about a year and a month ago? I was so grateful for the opportunity, I really was! I didn't want to complain or seem unappreciative, so I just tried to push through. But yeah, that's when things started feeling... heavy. I hope that's the kind of timeline you were looking for? I can try to be more specific if you need me to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these feelings of emptiness and lack of pleasure for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess so. I mean, I don't want to sound like I'm just sitting around moping all day, because I really do try to stay positive! I try to put on a good face for everyone at work, and I make sure I get my tasks done. \n\nBut... underneath that, yeah, it's pretty much there all the time. From when I wake up until I go to sleep, things just feel... flat. Even when I'm smiling or trying to be helpful, it's like I'm just going through the motions. I hope it doesn't make me sound lazy or ungrateful for the good things in my life. Is it normal to feel like that all day? I can try to focus more on the positives if you think that would help.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these feelings of emptiness and lack of pleasure for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, yes... definitely. Like I was saying about the prototype role, it's been going on for over a year now, so definitely longer than two weeks. I'm sorry if I wasn't clear about that a second ago! \n\nI really don't want to sound like a broken record or like I'm just complaining all the time. I know I should have probably said something sooner instead of letting it go on for so many months, and I really am trying my best to snap out of it. But yes, to answer your question, it's been way more than two weeks. Is that... is that the right information you needed? I can try to explain it differently if that wasn't helpful.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past year?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I don't think so, anyway. I mean, my clothes still fit about the same, I think? I try to make sure I'm still eating, even when I don't really have an appetite, because I know it's important to keep my strength up for work. I don't want anyone to worry about me or think I'm not taking care of myself. \n\nI might have fluctuated a pound or two here and there, just from being busy, but nothing major. I hope that's okay? Is it bad if I haven't lost weight? I can try to pay closer attention to it if you need me to.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these feelings of emptiness and lack of pleasure are interfering with your ability to function at work or in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really try my best not to let it affect my job, because I never want to be a burden to my team. They're all so great. But... I guess, if I'm being completely honest, it has been a little tough lately. \n\nI actually... well, I missed a key project deadline recently. I felt so awful about it! And lately, I've been needing my coworkers to kind of double-check or even take over parts of the reports I usually finish completely on my own. I hope my boss isn't too disappointed in me, I really am trying hard to get back up to speed so I can pull my weight again. \n\nAnd outside of work... I guess I've mostly stopped seeing people or doing the daily routines that used to keep me steady. I just don't want to bring my friends down or be bad company, you know? Does that make sense? I'm really trying to do better and get back on track, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling empty, sad, or without pleasure for more days than not over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um... well, like I mentioned before, it's actually been going on for over a year now, so yes, it's definitely been more days than not lately. I hope it's okay that I brought up the year-long timeline again? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture. \n\nI really do try to put on a brave face and push through it most days, especially at work, because I don't want anyone to think I'm just giving up. But underneath it all... yes, that empty feeling is pretty much always there. Is that the kind of detail you were looking for? I can try to be more specific if you need me to.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that these empty feelings and lack of pleasure are significantly impacting your daily functioning and relationships?\n\nPatient: Um... I know we touched on this a little bit just a moment ago, and I hope it's okay that I'm kind of repeating myself... I just want to make sure I'm answering you correctly. \n\nBut, yes... I guess they are. I really do try so hard to keep everything together, but... like I said, I did miss that big project deadline recently. And I feel terrible about it. I've had to ask my coworkers to double-check my reports, or even take over sections that I used to just breeze through on my own. I hate being a bother to them, they're so busy themselves. \n\nAnd then, outside of the office... I've just kind of stopped doing the things that used to keep me on track. I haven't really been seeing my friends much either. I just don't want to drag them down with my mood, you know? I really am trying to push past it, I promise. Does that answer your question okay? I can give you more examples if you need them.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any thoughts of death or suicidal ideation recently?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I want to be really clear that I would never actually do anything to hurt myself. I know how much that would devastate my family, and I could never put them through that. I'm really trying to stay positive. \n\nBut... I guess, if I'm being completely open with you... sometimes, when things feel really heavy... like on my commute to work... I catch myself having these passing thoughts. Just... thinking that maybe it would be easier if I just didn't arrive anywhere. It's just a silly thought, really. But sometimes the idea of just... steering off the road pops into my head, and it actually scares me enough that I have to grip the wheel really tight, or even pull over for a minute to catch my breath. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that sounds terrible. I promise I'm safe, and I'm really trying to work through these feelings. Is it normal to have scary thoughts like that when you're feeling so down? I just want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information so you can help me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these feelings of emptiness and lack of pleasure nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes, I suppose so. I mean, I really do try to find the silver lining in things, and I don't want to sound like I'm just complaining all the time. But... if I'm being honest, yes, it's pretty much every day. \n\nEven when I'm at work and I'm smiling and trying to be helpful to my team, that... that heavy, blank feeling is still just sort of sitting there underneath it all. I really hope I don't sound like I'm just wallowing in it, because I truly am trying my best to stay busy and push through. Does that make sense?     "psychosocial_stressor": null,
```json
    "additional_requirements": [
      "Onset of symptoms after starting the new role at work",
      "Persistent feelings of emptiness and lack of pleasure nearly every day",
      "Significant impairment in social and occupational functioning"
    ]
  }
}
```
